```
30	theorem runFuel_eq_stepN (halt : Word) (K : Nat) (m : State)
31	    (hne : ∀ j, j < K → (stepN j m).pc ≠ halt) :
32	    runFuel halt K m = stepN K m := by
33	  induction K generalizing m with

35	  | succ K ih =>

37	      have hstep : ∀ j, j < K → (stepN j (step m)).pc ≠ halt := fun j hj => by

39	      rw [runFuel, if_neg h0, ih (step m) hstep, stepN_succ]
```

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  18:32:02; tool 76ms

```
name = "hex0"
defaultTargets = ["Spec", "RawAsm", "LowIR", "LowSSA"]

# The language tower is one library per top-level directory. Each lib is rooted
# at an aggregator module (e.g. `RawAsm.lean` imports the rung's main entry
# points) and claims its whole namespace subtree with a submodule glob
# ("Ns.*"), so every module is claimed by exactly one lib (the disjoint
# top-level namespaces guarantee no overlap). Cross-lib import dependencies
# (RawAsm → Spec, LowIR → Spec/RawAsm, LowSSA → LowIR) are resolved by Lake's
# global module graph. Build a whole rung with `lake build <Lib>`, or a single
# module with `lake build <Module.Name>`.
#
#   Spec/     — functional specifications, the refinement targets (rung-independent)
#   RawAsm/   — bare RV64I model + hex0/hex1 verified at the flat-PC altitude
#   LowIR/    — structured IL (Core → Ctrl → Prog) + verified compiler + ProgSim
#   LowSSA/   — the SSA variant of the Prog IR (docs/LOWIR-SSA-EXPERIMENT.md)
#
# (`lean/DumpProgMain.lean` lives outside every namespace on purpose — it is a
# `lake env lean --run` tool, not a library module, so no lib claims it.)

[[lean_lib]]
name = "Spec"
globs = ["Spec.*"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "RawAsm"
globs = ["RawAsm.*"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIR"
globs = ["LowIR.*"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowSSA"
globs = ["LowSSA.*"]

# --- back-compat target aliases -------------------------------------------
# The four libs above ARE the tower (and the default build). The aliases below
# preserve the pre-reorg `lake build <name>` invocations documented in the
# handoff/experiment docs. They are NOT in defaultTargets; each roots a subset
# of the tower, and its cross-subtree dependencies resolve through the glob
# libs above (Lake builds a single global module graph). Overlapping claims are
# fine — a module builds once and both libs share the olean.

[[lean_lib]]
name = "Hex0"                       # the hex0 rung program (was lean/Hex0/*)
roots = ["RawAsm.Hex0"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "Hex1"                       # the hex1 rung program (was lean/Hex1/*)
roots = ["RawAsm.Hex1"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIRCompile"               # the D7/D8 compiler cut + differential tests
roots = ["LowIR.CompileTests"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIRProgSim"               # the compile_sim-for-Prog proof campaign
roots = ["LowIR.ProgSim.Defs", "LowIR.ProgSim.SlotFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.MemFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.StmtSim", "LowIR.ProgSim.CtrlSim", "LowIR.ProgSim.EncodeFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.AsmFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.LowerFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.LayoutFacts", "LowIR.ProgSim.Main"]

[[lean_lib]]
name = "LowIRSSA"                   # the SSA experiment rung
roots = ["LowSSA.Dump", "LowSSA.ExecFacts", "LowSSA.Strlen.Proof", "LowSSA.Hex0.Proof"]
```

```
30	open LowIR.Prog (Reg Name FunDef Env Data Program frameEnter
31	                 dataSegment dataOffsetsFrom pad8 dbaseOf installData)
32	open LowIR.Compile (compileProgT progBytes userOff totalFrame slotOff maxRegF)
33	open Rv64i (Word Byte Instr State)
34	
35	-- `St`/`exec`/`Stmt` collide with the Ctrl-level `LowIR.St`/`LowIR.exec`
36	-- (enclosing-namespace lookup wins over `open LowIR.Prog`), so alias the Prog
37	-- ones locally and qualify the rest.
38	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
39	abbrev St := LowIR.Prog.St

41	/-! ## §3.1 (part) — where the code and data live: `Layout` and `Installed` -/
42	
43	/-- The compiled artifact's placement, per RESUME-PROGSIM §3.1: the base
44	    address, the resolved instruction stream (`= (compileProgT P entry).1`), the
45	    function→byte-offset table, the byte offset where the data segment starts
46	    inside the blob (`pad8 (4 * #instrs)` — every instruction is 4 bytes after
47	    resolution), and the program's const data. -/
48	structure Layout where
49	  codeBase : Word
50	  instrs   : List Instr
51	  fnTab    : List (Name × Nat)
52	  segStart : Nat
53	  data     : Data
54	  stackLo  : Word          -- the stack floor (a fixed per-run constant, like
55	                           -- `codeBase`); the free stack `[stackLo, sp)` below the
56	                           -- current frame is machine-private (C2 — see `StInv`).
57	deriving Repr
58	
59	/-- Build the `Layout` for `entry` at `codeBase` straight from the FROZEN
60	    compiler. `none` iff the program is uncompilable (same condition as
61	    `compileProgT`). `segStart` is recomputed here from the resolved stream
62	    and is DEFINITIONALLY the compiler's `pad8 codeEnd` (proved in Phase 2). -/
63	def layoutOf (P : Program) (entry : Name) (codeBase stackLo : Word) : Option Layout :=
64	  match compileProgT P entry with
65	  | some (is, fns, _) =>
66	      some { codeBase, instrs := is, fnTab := fns,
67	             segStart := pad8 (4 * is.length), data := P.data, stackLo }
68	  | none => none
69	
70	/-- The machine halt pc: the self-loop landing pad the compiler puts at
71	    `codeBase+4` (the entry stub is `jal ra,entry` at `codeBase`; the entry
72	    returns to `codeBase+4`). Run the machine with `runFuel L.haltPc`. -/
73	def Layout.haltPc (L : Layout) : Word := L.codeBase + 4
74	
75	/-- Address one past the last instruction — the code window's top. -/
76	def Layout.codeEnd (L : Layout) : Word :=
77	  L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (4 * L.instrs.length)
78
```

```
858	/-! ## `MemAccOff` — the memory-access side condition for `lower_sim`. -/
859	
860	/-- Every byte the statement's IL memory ops touch lies off `MachPriv` (the frame
861	    holes + code blob). This is the read+write generalisation of Defs' footprint
862	    note (`ws.all (¬ MachPriv ·)`) — loads need it for value agreement, stores for
863	    slot/`Installed` preservation. It mirrors `exec`'s recursion for `seq` (the
864	    second statement's addresses depend on the first's result); `True` for the
865	    non-memory and (still-`sorry`) control-flow cases. `lbu`/`sb` touch one byte,
866	    `ld`/`sd` eight; both `n`-byte ranges are pinned via `¬ MachPriv (base + i)`. -/
867	def MemAccOff (L : Layout) (holes : List Hole)
868	    (P : Program) (dbase : Name → Option Word) (pad : Name → Nat) (stackLo : Word) :
869	    Nat → PStmt → St → Prop
870	  | _, .ld _ rs imm, s =>
871	      ∀ i, i < 8 → OffPriv L holes s.sp (s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64 + BitVec.ofNat 64 i)
872	  | _, .lbu _ rs imm, s => OffPriv L holes s.sp (s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64)
873	  | _, .sd rb _ imm, s =>
874	      (s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64).toNat + 8 ≤ 2 ^ 64 ∧
875	        ∀ i, i < 8 → OffPriv L holes s.sp (s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64 + BitVec.ofNat 64 i)
876	  | _, .sb rb _ imm, s => OffPriv L holes s.sp (s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64)
877	  | fuel + 1, .seq a b, s =>
878	      MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel a s ∧
879	        (∀ s1, LowIR.Prog.exec P dbase pad stackLo fuel a s = some (s1, .normal) →
880	           MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel b s1)
881	  | fuel + 1, .block body, s =>
882	      MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel body s
883	  | fuel + 1, .ife _ _ _ t e, s =>
884	      MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel t s ∧
885	        MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel e s
886	  | fuel + 1, .while c a b body, s =>
887	      -- one unrolling: the body runs at `fuel`, and every iteration that loops
888	      -- back (`.normal`/`.cont 0`) must itself be access-safe — mirrors `exec`'s
889	      -- self-recursion, fuel-structural exactly like `seq`.
890	      MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel body s ∧
891	        (∀ s', (LowIR.Prog.exec P dbase pad stackLo fuel body s = some (s', .normal) ∨
892	                LowIR.Prog.exec P dbase pad stackLo fuel body s = some (s', .cont 0)) →
893	           MemAccOff L holes P dbase pad stackLo fuel (.while c a b body) s')
894	  | fuel + 1, .call _ _ f args _, s =>
895	      -- C6: a call is access-safe iff the CALLEE's body is access-safe from its

905	      | none => True
906	  | _, _, _ => True
```

```
300	
301	/-- Every label position fits the 21-bit `jal`/branch offset window (from byte 0,
302	    so target − here ∈ (−2²⁰, 2²⁰) whenever both ends are `< 2²⁰`). -/
303	def LabelsOk (brkPos contPos : List Nat) (epiPos : Nat) : Prop :=
304	  (∀ p ∈ brkPos, p < 2 ^ 20) ∧ (∀ p ∈ contPos, p < 2 ^ 20) ∧ epiPos < 2 ^ 20
305	
306	/-- Every `ife`'s else-skip branch fits the 13-bit branch window (`8 + 4·csize e`
307	    = the compiler's `resolveOne` guard `δ ≤ 4094`). `while`'s branch is a fixed
308	    `+8`, always fine. A structural side condition, discharged per-program by the
309	    real layout (branches that don't fit make `Compile` return `none`). -/
310	def BranchOk : PStmt → Prop
311	  | .seq a b          => BranchOk a ∧ BranchOk b
312	  | .block body       => BranchOk body
313	  | .ife _ _ _ t e    => 8 + 4 * csize e < 2 ^ 12 ∧ BranchOk t ∧ BranchOk e
314	  | .while _ _ _ body => BranchOk body
315	  | _                 => True
316	
317	/-- A conditional branch `condInstr c T0 T1 δ` whose registers hold `x`, `y`: when
318	    `evalCond c x y` holds the branch is taken (`pc += δ`), matching the compiler's
319	    positive-form encoding (`.ge`/`.geu` take the *else* arm of `bge`/`bgeu`). -/
320	theorem cond_taken (m : State) (c : Cond) (δ : Int) (x y : Word)
321	    (hd : decode (fetch32 m) = condInstr c T0 T1 δ)
322	    (hx : m.rget T0 = x) (hy : m.rget T1 = y) (hev : evalCond c x y = true) :
323	    step m = m.setPc (m.pc + (BitVec.ofInt 13 δ).signExtend 64) := by
324	  cases c <;> simp only [condInstr] at hd
325	  · have h : x = y := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
326	    rw [step_beq m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_pos h]
327	  · have h : x.slt y = true := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
328	    rw [step_blt m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_pos h]
329	  · have h : x.slt y = false := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
330	    rw [step_bge m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_neg (by simp [h])]
331	  · have h : x.ult y = false := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
332	    rw [step_bgeu m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_neg (by simp [h])]
333	
334	/-- …and when `evalCond c x y` fails the branch falls through (`pc += 4`). -/
335	theorem cond_not_taken (m : State) (c : Cond) (δ : Int) (x y : Word)
336	    (hd : decode (fetch32 m) = condInstr c T0 T1 δ)
337	    (hx : m.rget T0 = x) (hy : m.rget T1 = y) (hev : evalCond c x y = false) :
338	    step m = m.setPc (m.pc + 4) := by
339	  cases c <;> simp only [condInstr] at hd
```

```
3	  (RESUME-PROGSIM §3.2, Phase 4). THE VERTICAL SLICE: this file states the whole
4	  relation (`emit`/`Emitted`/`lower_sim`) and proves the straight-line cases
5	  (skip/annot/arith ops/**memory ops**/seq); only the control-flow cases remain
6	  `sorry`'d (Phase 4.3). It is the go/no-go for the `StInv`-based relation design — if
7	  the plain-equality relation is wrong, it is wrong HERE, on a 4-instruction
8	  slice, cheaply.
9	
10	  `emit` is the RESOLVED straight-line lowering (mirrors `Compile.lower` for the
11	  label-free cases, with `loadSlot`/`storeSlot` already resolved to concrete
12	  `Instr`s — every such `SymInstr` is `.ins`, position-independent). `Emitted L
13	  pos is` is the STATIC fact that `L.instrs` carries `is` at byte offset `pos`;
14	  combined with the `Installed` conjunct of `StInv` (preserved across steps), it
15	  yields the machine fetch. The Phase-2 connection `Emitted L pos (emit stmt)`
16	  from the real `layout`/`resolveOne` pipeline is deferred (AsmFacts).
17	-/
18	import LowIR.ProgSim.MemFacts
19	
20	namespace LowIR.ProgSim
21	
22	open LowIR.Prog (Name FunDef Program dbaseOf)
23	open LowIR.Compile (userOff slotOff maxRegF maxRegS SP T0 T1)
24	open Rv64i (Word Byte State Instr fetch32 decode step)
25	
26	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
27	
28	/-! ## Plain machine iteration `stepN` (no early-halt check — `Nat.iterate` and
29	    the `f^[n]` notation are Mathlib-only in this toolchain). A top-level bridge
30	    to `runFuel (codeBase+4)` lands in Phase 6; mid-proof we use `stepN`. -/
31	def stepN : Nat → State → State
32	  | 0,     m => m
33	  | k + 1, m => stepN k (step m)
34	
35	@[simp] theorem stepN_zero (m : State) : stepN 0 m = m := rfl
36	theorem stepN_succ (k : Nat) (m : State) : stepN (k + 1) m = stepN k (step m) := rfl
37	
38	/-- Iteration composes: `a` then `b` steps. Induction on `a` (the `step m` peels
39	    from the front, matching `stepN`'s definitional recursion). -/
40	theorem stepN_add (a b : Nat) (m : State) : stepN (a + b) m = stepN b (stepN a m) := by
41	  induction a generalizing m with
42	  | zero => rw [Nat.zero_add]; rfl
43	  | succ a ih => rw [show a + 1 + b = (a + b) + 1 from by omega, stepN_succ, stepN_succ, ih]
44	
45	/-! ## The resolved straight-line lowering `emit`. -/
46	
47	/-- Resolved `loadSlot`: physical reg `t := IL reg r` (r=0 ⇒ materialise 0). -/
48	def loadSlotI (r t : Nat) : List Instr :=
49	  if r = 0 then [.addi t 0 0] else [.ld t SP (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r))]
50	
51	/-- Resolved `storeSlot`: IL reg `r := physical reg t` (r=0 ⇒ discard). -/
52	def storeSlotI (r t : Nat) : List Instr :=
53	  if r = 0 then [] else [.sd SP t (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r))]
54	
55	/-- The resolved instruction stream for a straight-line statement — mirrors
```

```
    (layout segs pos).2.2.2 = pos + totalSymSize (segs.flatMap Prod.snd) := by
```

```json
{"replace_all": false, "file_path": "/var/data/bootstrap/lean/LowIR/ProgSim/StmtSim.lean", "old_string": "/-- `NoHalt` composes: `k` safe steps from `m`, the boundary pc at step `k` safe,\n    then `k'` safe steps from `stepN k m`, give `k + k'` safe steps from `m`. -/\ntheorem NoHalt_chain (L : Layout) (k k' : Nat) (m : State)\n    (h1 : NoHalt L k m)\n    (hb : (stepN k m).pc ≠ L.codeBase + 4)\n    (h2 : NoHalt L k' (stepN k m)) :\n    NoHalt L (k + k') m := by\n  intro j hj\n  rcases Nat.lt_or_ge j k with hjk | hjk\n  · exact h1 j hjk\n  · rw [show j = k + (j - k) from by omega, stepN_add]\n    rcases Nat.eq_zero_or_pos (j - k) with h0 | hpos\n    · rw [h0, stepN_zero]; exact hb\n    · exact h2 (j - k) (by omega)", "new_string": "/-- `NoHalt` composes: `k` safe steps from `m`, then `k'` safe steps from\n    `stepN k m`, give `k + k'` safe steps from `m`. 92	/-! ## The resolved straight-line lowering `emit`. -/
```
```
93	
94	/-- Resolved `loadSlot`: physical reg `t := IL reg r` (r=0 ⇒ materialise 0). -/
95	def loadSlotI (r t : Nat) : List Instr :=
96	  if r = 0 then [.addi t 0 0] else [.ld t SP (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r))]
97	
98	/-- Resolved `storeSlot`: IL reg `r := physical reg t` (r=0 ⇒ discard). -/
99	def storeSlotI (r t : Nat) : List Instr :=
100	  if r = 0 then [] else [.sd SP t (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r))]
101	
102	/-- The resolved instruction stream for a straight-line statement — mirrors
103	    `Compile.lower` exactly for the label-free cases (validated by `#guard`
104	    against the real pipeline below). Control-flow/`cref`/`clen` are `[]` here;
105	    their `lower_sim` cases are `sorry` (they use the real `Emitted`, not `emit`). -/
106	def emit : PStmt → List Instr
107	  | .skip            => []
108	  | .annot _         => []
109	  | .addi rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.addi T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
110	  | .add  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.add T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
111	  | .sub  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.sub T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
112	  | .orr  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.or  T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
113	  | .slli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.slli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
114	  | .srli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.srli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
115	  | .lbu  rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.lbu T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
116	  | .ld   rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.ld  T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
117	  | .sb   rb rv imm  => loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [.sb T0 T1 imm]
118	  | .sd   rb rv imm  => loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [.sd T0 T1 imm]
119	  | .seq a b         => emit a ++ emit b
120	  | _                => []
121
```

```
518	/-! ## The prologue simulator (`prologue_sim`, RESUME-CALL W5).
519	
520	    The machine prologue (`prologueI fd`) transforms a CALL-ENTRY machine state
521	    (x2 = the caller's sp, `a0..` = the argument values, ra = the return address)
522	    into the callee's entry state: it drops sp by `totalFrame` (= `userOff +
523	    frameSize`), saves ra at `[sp', sp'+8)`, parks the params from `a0..`,
524	    zero-inits every other register slot, and materializes the user-frame base
525	    into `frameReg`'s slot. We show the resulting machine state satisfies the
526	    callee's `StInv` against `frameEnter`'s IL state (with the P1 pad `= userOff`).
527	
528	    The heart is the last-wins agreement between the machine's sequential param
529	    stores and the IL's `withParams` left fold: both write slot `params[i]` the
530	    value `argVals[i]` in order, so the last write to any register wins on both
531	    sides. We capture this with `parkFold` — literally `frameEnter`'s fold — and
532	    show the machine memory tracks it pointwise on slots, off a shifted base. -/
533	
534	/-- The IL's `withParams` left fold, factored out (matches `frameEnter` verbatim):
```

```
114	def execT (P : Program) (dbase : Name → Option Word) (pad : Name → Nat)
115	    (stackLo : Word) :
116	    Nat → PStmt → St → Option (St × Outcome × List Word)
117	  | 0,      _,    _ => none
118	  | fuel+1, stmt, s =>
119	    match stmt with
120	    | .skip            => some (s, .normal, [])
121	    | .annot _         => some (s, .normal, [])
122	    | .cref rd d       =>
123	        match dbase d with
124	        | some a => some (s.rset rd a, .normal, [])
125	        | none   => none
126	    | .clen rd d       =>
127	        match List.lookup d P.data with
128	        | some bs => some (s.rset rd (BitVec.ofNat 64 bs.length), .normal, [])
129	        | none    => none
130	    | .addi rd rs imm  => some (s.rset rd (s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64), .normal, [])
131	    | .add  rd r1 r2   => some (s.rset rd (s.rget r1 + s.rget r2), .normal, [])
132	    | .sub  rd r1 r2   => some (s.rset rd (s.rget r1 - s.rget r2), .normal, [])
133	    | .orr  rd r1 r2   => some (s.rset rd (s.rget r1 ||| s.rget r2), .normal, [])
134	    | .slli rd rs sh   => some (s.rset rd (s.rget rs <<< sh), .normal, [])
135	    | .srli rd rs sh   => some (s.rset rd (s.rget rs >>> sh), .normal, [])
136	    | .lbu  rd rs imm  => let a := s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64
137	                          some (s.rset rd ((s.loadByte a).setWidth 64), .normal, [])
138	    | .sb   rb rv imm  => let a := s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64
139	                          some (s.storeByte a ((s.rget rv).setWidth 8), .normal, [a])
140	    | .ld   rd rs imm  => let a := s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64

142	    | .sd   rb rv imm  => let a := s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64

144	                                [a, a+1, a+2, a+3, a+4, a+5, a+6, a+7])
```

```
596	    `synthConst` sequence (`v = hi*4096 + lo`, both 12-bit signed): `addi t 0 hi;

598	    and `t = ofInt 64 v` — the reusable core of the `clen` case and half of `cref`. -/
```

```
34:- The worked assembly template: `case call` (CtrlSim.lean:2159) — segs 1–6.

45:## 1. Ground truth — what happens at the top level

48:`Installed`, memory = zeros + data off the blob):

51:   fnPosOf L entry`, **`ra := codeBase + 4` = `L.haltPc`** — the "caller" the
52:   entry function returns to is the halt pad itself.
53:2. **`entry`'s prologue** (`prologue_sim` at `holes := []`, `sp0`, `ra :=
54:   codeBase+4`): establishes `StInv` for `frameEnter`'s `st0` at holes
```

````
## 2. Statement repairs (S1–S4) — do FIRST, the current statement is not provable

Each is a hypothesis to add to `entry_run_sim` AND `prog_sim` (they share the
signature; `prog_sim` forwards). Re-green the `prog_sim` assembly after.

### S1 — tie `stackLo` to `L.stackLo`

The statement runs the IL at a free `stackLo` but builds the layout at
`L.stackLo` (`hL : layoutOf P entry L.codeBase L.stackLo = some L`), with no
relation between them. `lower_sim_cf` requires `hstackLo : stackLo =
L.stackLo` (its c4/`OffPriv` domain is stated in `L.stackLo`,
Defs.lean:400) — underivable today. Fix: **drop the free parameter — state
`run`/`SimPre`/`MachStack` at `L.stackLo` throughout** (or, minimally, add
`hslo : stackLo = L.stackLo`). Dropping the parameter is cleaner; `prog_sim`'s
public shape stays sensible because `layoutOf` copies `stackLo` verbatim
(`layoutOf_stackLo`, AsmFacts.lean:185).

### S2 — the footprint hypothesis `haccess` is MISSING

`lower_sim_cf` needs `MemAccOff` (StmtSim.lean:867) for the body — the §3.4
per-program footprint side condition. It is not derivable (an IL program that
stores into the blob genuinely breaks the simulation); it MUST be a
hypothesis. `st0` is internal to the proof (produced by `run_inv`), so state
it quantified, mirroring `MemAccOff`'s own call arm (C6):

```lean
(haccess : ∀ st0, frameEnter L.stackLo fd (userPad P.env entry) args
              (installData dataBase P.data (fun _ => 0)) sp0 = some st0 →
    MemAccOff L [(st0.sp, userOff fd)] P (dbaseOf dataBase P.data)
      (userPad P.env) L.stackLo fuel fd.body st0)
```

with `dataBase := L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 L.segStart` (abbreviate it in
the statement — it already appears twice). Per-program this is dischargeable
at the IL altitude exactly like the hex0 `storeByte_preserves` shape; for the
oracle programs it is `decide`-able.

### S3 — arity: add `hargc : args.length = fd.argc`

`run` (Prog.lean:438) has NO arity check — `frameEnter` zips
`fd.params.toList` with the raw `args`. `prologue_sim`'s `hlen` demands
`params.length = argVals.length`, and the machine parks ALL `argc` params. If
`args.length < fd.argc` the two last-wins folds genuinely diverge on
**duplicate params** (legal — `wfProgram`, Prog.lean:276, forbids only
frameReg-as-param): IL `parkFold [(p,v)]` vs machine `parkFold [(p,v),(p,0)]`.
So the theorem as stated is FALSE without `hargc`. With it, `argVals := args`
instantiates `prologue_sim` directly and `hargs`'s `args.getD i 0` rewrites to
`args[i]`. (Alternative — pad `argVals` and prove a `parkFold`-pad agreement
lemma under a params-nodup assumption — rejected: `wfProgram` doesn't give
nodup, and every real caller passes exact arity anyway.)

### S4 — `BranchOk` for all functions: add `hbr`

`fn_hfn` takes `hbr : ∀ g gd, List.lookup g P.env = some gd → BranchOk
gd.body` as an input (LayoutFacts.lean:1251) — it is not yet derived from the
compiler guard. Add the same hypothesis to `entry_run_sim`/`prog_sim`

range checks via `compileFun_resolves` and delete the hypothesis everywhere.
````

````
## 3. THE design piece: the `hne` retrofit ("NoHalt")

### 3.1 Why a retrofit and not a local argument

`hne` needs `pc ≠ codeBase+4` at EVERY step `j < K`. The alternatives:

- **Freeze argument** (no `hne` at all): if pc hits the halt pad early, the
  pad's `jal x0, 0` self-loop freezes the state, so `stepN K = stepN j` and
  `runFuel` agrees anyway. REJECTED: it needs `decode (fetch32 …) = jal0 0`
  at the *intermediate* state `stepN j m0` — i.e. per-step `Installed` — which
  is a retrofit of exactly the same width, delivering strictly less.
- **Per-step pc conjunct** — ADOPTED. Every `∃ k`-shaped lemma additionally
  concludes its intermediate pcs avoid the halt pad. Mechanically identical in
  shape to the C3 `FramesPres` retrofit (RESUME-CALL §2), which this codebase
  has already done once and priced.

### 3.2 The conjunct and its helpers (new, in `Defs.lean` or `StmtSim.lean`)

```lean
def NoHalt (L : Layout) (k : Nat) (m : State) : Prop :=
  ∀ j, j < k → (stepN j m).pc ≠ L.codeBase + 4
```

Strict `j < k`: the endpoint is excluded (each lemma's exact endpoint pc is a
separate conclusion already; only `entry_run_sim`'s FINAL endpoint is the halt
pad). Two helpers do all the composition:

- `pc_ne_halt : 8 ≤ q → q < 2 ^ 20 → L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 q ≠
  L.codeBase + 4` — cancel the add (`BitVec` add-left-cancel), then `ofNat q =
  ofNat 4 → q = 4` from `q < 2^20 < 2^64`, contradiction with `8 ≤ q`. Also
  the degenerate `L.codeBase ≠ L.codeBase + 4` for step 0 of the stub
  (`(4 : BitVec 64) ≠ 0`).
- `NoHalt_chain : NoHalt L k m → (stepN k m).pc ≠ L.codeBase + 4 →
  NoHalt L k' (stepN k m) → NoHalt L (k + k') m` — via `stepN_add`; the middle
  premise handles the boundary `j = k` and is one `pc_ne_halt` at the caller
  (every boundary pc is a known `codeBase + ofNat q`).

### 3.3 Position lower bounds: where `8 ≤ q` comes from

All function code sits after the 8-byte stub, so every position a running
program visits is `≥ 8` — but the theorems must SEE that:

- `lower_sim_cf` gains hypothesis **`hhere8 : 8 ≤ here`** (sibling of
  `hhere4`). Self-propagates to every sub-position (`here + 4·…`).
- **`LabelsOk` strengthens** (CtrlSim.lean:303): each `p ∈ brkPos/contPos` and
  `epiPos` gets `8 ≤ p ∧ p < 2^20` (jump landings at labels are intermediate
  pcs of the enclosing statement). Construction sites: `block`/`while` push
  positions derived from `here` (≥ 8 by `hhere8`); the `call` case passes
  `[]`/`[]`/`epiPos'` with `epiPos' ≥ fnPos g ≥ 8` (below).
- **`fn_hfn` gains conjunct 7: `8 ≤ fnPosOf L g`** (equivalently a standalone
  `fnPosOf_ge8`). Proof from the existing decomp: `fnPosOf L g =
  4·preF.flatten.length` (`fnPosOf_tie`) and `preF` starts with the resolved
  stub segment, whose flatten has length 2 (`stub_emitted`'s `hflat`
  machinery) ⇒ `≥ 8`. Thread into `hfn`'s statement in `lower_sim_cf` and the
  call case's IH instance.

### 3.4 Retrofit inventory (each lemma: conclusion gains `NoHalt`, plus
`8 ≤ p` / end-bound hypotheses where the lemma doesn't already have them)

Straight-line atoms prove it by enumerating their per-step pcs (which their
proofs already compute explicitly — `hPCld`-style facts) + `pc_ne_halt`;
compound lemmas chain sub-conclusions with `NoHalt_chain`.
````

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  20:17:33; tool 5.2s

```
=== run_inv ===
theorem run_inv {P : Program} {stackLo : Word} {fuel : Nat} {f : Name}
    {args : List Word} {mem : Word → Byte} {sp0 : Word} {pad : Name → Nat}
    {dataBase : Word} {s' : St}
    (h : run P stackLo fuel f args mem sp0 pad dataBase = some s') :
    ∃ (fd : FunDef) (st0 : St) (oc : Outcome),
      List.lookup f P.env = some fd
      ∧ frameEnter stackLo fd (pad f) args (installData dataBase P.data mem) sp0 = some st0
      ∧ exec P (dbaseOf dataBase P.data) pad stackLo fuel fd.body st0 = some (s', oc)
      ∧ (oc = Outcome.normal ∨ oc = Outcome.ret) := by
```

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  20:17:42; tool 2.7s

```
=== fn_hfn ===
theorem fn_hfn {P : Program} {entry : Name} {cb slo : Word} {L : Layout}
    {dats : List (Name × Nat)}
    (hL : layoutOf P entry cb slo = some L)
    (hc : compileProgT P entry = some (L.instrs, L.fnTab, dats))
    (hcode : 4 * L.instrs.length < 2 ^ 20)
    (hbr : ∀ g gd, List.lookup g P.env = some gd → BranchOk gd.body)
    (g : Name) (gd : FunDef) (hlk : List.lookup g P.env = some gd) :
    Emitted L (fnPosOf L g)
        (prologueI gd
          ++ emitCF P.data (dposOf L) (fnPosOf L) [] []
               (fnPosOf L g + 4 * prologueSize gd + 4 * csize gd.body)
               (fnPosOf L g + 4 * prologueSize gd) gd.body
          ++ epilogueI gd)
      ∧ fnPosOf L g + 4 * prologueSize gd + 4 * csize gd.body + 4 * epilogueSize gd < 2 ^ 20
      ∧ BranchOk gd.body
      ∧ totalFrame gd ≤ 2000
      ∧ gd.frameSize % 8 = 0
      ∧ fnPosOf L g % 4 = 0
      ∧ 8 ≤ fnPosOf L g := by
```

```
=== SimPre def ===
structure SimPre (L : Layout) (stackLo sp0 : Word) : Prop where
  spAligned    : sp0.toNat % 8 = 0
  stackNonEmpty : stackLo.toNat ≤ sp0.toNat
  blobBelowStack : L.codeBase.toNat + L.blobLen ≤ stackLo.toNat  -- code+data placed below the stack
  codeAligned  : L.codeBase.toNat % 4 = 0       -- `halign`: the loader places code 4-aligned
  blobFits     : L.blobLen < 2 ^ 20             -- the code+data blob fits in 1 MiB (⇒ `hdpos`, `hcode`)
```

```
theorem stub_emitted {P : Program} {entry : Name} {cb slo : Word} {L : Layout}
    {dats : List (Name × Nat)}
    (hL : layoutOf P entry cb slo = some L)
    (hc : compileProgT P entry = some (L.instrs, L.fnTab, dats)) :
    Emitted L 0 [Instr.jal RA (BitVec.ofInt 21 (fnPosOf L entry : Int)), jal0 0] := by
  -- entry is a real, non-empty function name (guards)
  obtain ⟨fd, hlk⟩ := compileProgT_entry hc
  obtain ⟨preE, sufE, hEE, _⟩ := lookup_split entry P.env fd hlk
  have hmemE : (entry, fd) ∈ P.env := by rw [hEE]; simp
  obtain ⟨_, hentry, _, _⟩ := fn_guard_facts hc hmemE
  -- decompose the resolve; peel the stub prefix off progLayout.1
  obtain ⟨rs, hrs, hins, hfnt, _⟩ := compileProgT_decomp hc
  have hpl1 : (progLayout P entry).1
      = (layoutItems (stubSeg entry) 0).1
        ++ (layout (mapSegs P.data P.env 0).1 (layoutItems (stubSeg entry) 0).2.2).1 := rfl
  rw [hpl1] at hrs
  obtain ⟨rsStub, rsRest, hStub, _, hsplit⟩ := mapM_append_inv _ _ _ _ hrs
  -- split the stub segment into its two items
  obtain ⟨ra, rb, hra, hrb, hflat⟩ :=
    resolve_flatten_append _ _ _ [SymInstr.callf entry] [SymInstr.ins (jal0 0)] 0 rsStub hStub
  -- second item: `.ins (jal0 0)` → `[jal0 0]`
  obtain ⟨vb, hvb, hrbEq⟩ := mapM_single_nonlabel _ _ _ _ _ rb (by simp) hrb
=== layoutOf_decomp ===
theorem layoutOf_decomp {P : Program} {entry : Name} {cb slo : Word} {L : Layout}
    (h : layoutOf P entry cb slo = some L) :
    L.codeBase = cb ∧ L.stackLo = slo ∧ L.data = P.data
    ∧ L.segStart = pad8 (4 * L.instrs.length)
    ∧ ∃ dats, compileProgT P entry = some (L.instrs, L.fnTab, dats) := by
  unfold layoutOf at h
  split at h
  · rename_i is fns dats' hc
    simp only [Option.some.injEq] at h
    subst h
    exact ⟨rfl, rfl, rfl, rfl, dats', hc⟩
  · exact absurd h (by simp)
```

```
theorem userPad_eq (env : Env) (g : Name) (gd : FunDef)
    (h : List.lookup g env = some gd) : userPad env g = userOff gd := by
  simp only [userPad, h, Option.elim]

/-! ## §5 The remaining flat obligations, discharged from `layoutOf`/the guard.

    `lower_sim_cf`/`prog_sim` take the flat compile-time hypotheses `hdat`,
    `hstackLo`, `hdbase`, `hdpos`, `halign`, `hfn` (RESUME-CALL §C4, RESUME-PROGSIM
    §Phase-2). This section closes the three that follow from `layoutOf` and the
    compiler's own data guard alone: `hdat` (clen synth range), `hstackLo` (the
    stackLo field), and `hdbase` (the data-address correspondence). `hdpos`
    (needs a blob-size bound), `halign` (codeBase alignment — a `prog_sim`
=== dbaseOf_dposOf ===
theorem dbaseOf_dposOf (L : Layout) (d : Name) (a : Word)
    (h : dbaseOf (L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 L.segStart) L.data d = some a) :
    a = L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (dposOf L d) := by
  unfold dbaseOf dposOf at *
  cases ho : List.lookup d (dataOffsetsFrom 0 L.data) with
  | none => rw [ho] at h; exact absurd h (by simp)
  | some off =>
    rw [ho] at h; simp only [Option.map_some, Option.some.injEq] at h
    have hshift : List.lookup d (dataOffsetsFrom L.segStart L.data)
        = (List.lookup d (dataOffsetsFrom 0 L.data)).map (L.segStart + ·) := by
      have := dataOffsetsFrom_shift (n := d) L.segStart 0 L.data
      simpa using this
    rw [hshift, ho]

=== clen_synthOk ===
theorem clen_synthOk {P : Program} (hwf : P.data.all (fun d => d.2.length < 2 ^ 22)) (d : Name) :
    -2048 ≤ synthHi (((List.lookup d P.data).map (·.length)).getD 0)
    ∧ synthHi (((List.lookup d P.data).map (·.length)).getD 0) ≤ 2047 := by
  cases h : List.lookup d P.data with
  | none =>
    simp only [Option.map_none, Option.getD_none]
    unfold synthHi synthLo; constructor <;> omega
  | some bs =>
    have hb : (bs.length : Nat) < 2 ^ 22 := by
      rcases lookup_len_lt P.data (2 ^ 22) d hwf with hl | hn
      · rw [h] at hl; simpa using hl
      · rw [h] at hn; exact absurd hn (by simp)
    simp only [Option.map_some, Option.getD_some]
=== compileProgT_dataBound ===
theorem compileProgT_dataBound {P : Program} {entry : Name} {t}
    (h : compileProgT P entry = some t) : P.data.all (fun d => d.2.length < 2 ^ 22) := by
  unfold compileProgT at h
  cases hg : (LowIR.Prog.wfProgram P && P.env.all (fun nf => LowIR.Compile.fnOk nf.2)
       && (List.lookup entry P.env).isSome
       && P.data.all (fun d => d.2.length < 2 ^ 22)) with
  | false => rw [hg] at h; simp at h
  | true => simp only [Bool.and_eq_true] at hg; exact hg.2

/-- **`hdat`.** For any program whose data satisfies the compiler guard, every
    object's `clen` length synthesizes within the 12-bit signed immediate window
    (`-2048 ≤ synthHi ≤ 2047`) — so `run_synth` fires. -/
=== codeLen_lt ===
theorem codeLen_lt (L : Layout) (hseg : 4 * L.instrs.length ≤ L.segStart)
    (hblob : L.blobLen < 2 ^ 20) : 4 * L.instrs.length < 2 ^ 20 :=
  Nat.lt_of_le_of_lt (codeLen_le_blobLen L hseg) hblob
```

```
=== defs ===
def MachStack (stackLo sp0 : Word) (a : Word) : Prop :=
  memRange a stackLo (sp0.toNat - stackLo.toNat)

/-- **C2 — `OffPriv L holes sp a`**: `a` is neither machine-private (blob + live
    holes) NOR in the free stack `[stackLo, sp)` below the current frame. This is
    the domain on which IL and machine memory agree (`StInv`), and the region an
    IL memory op must stay inside (`MemAccOff`). The free-stack carve-out is what
    makes the caller's invariant RESTORABLE after a call returns: the machine
    dirties the callee's `[ra][slots]` there, and the IL never wrote it — but it
    is below `sp`, so it is outside `OffPriv` and agreement is not demanded. -/
def OffPriv (L : Layout) (holes : List Hole) (sp a : Word) : Prop :=
  ¬ MachPriv L holes a ∧ ¬ memRange a L.stackLo (sp.toNat - L.stackLo.toNat)

/-- **`StInv L fd holes s m`** — the per-statement simulation invariant
    (RESUME-PROGSIM §3.1). At every IL statement boundary during `fd`'s body:
    `sp ≡ x2`; each live IL register sits in its 8-byte machine slot at
    `sp + slotOff r`; the code+data is installed; IL and machine memory agree
    off the machine-private bytes; and the current activation's hole is
    `[sp, sp + userOff fd)` with `sp` 8-aligned. -/
def StInv (L : Layout) (fd : FunDef) (holes : List Hole) (s : St) (m : State) : Prop :=
LowIR/ProgSim/Defs.lean:72:    returns to `codeBase+4`). Run the machine with `runFuel L.haltPc`. -/
LowIR/ProgSim/Defs.lean:73:def Layout.haltPc (L : Layout) : Word := L.codeBase + 4
```

```
- **`run_inv`** (ExecFacts, PROVEN, axiom-clean) — the IL-side inversion:

  fd.body st0 = some (s', oc) ∧ oc ∈ {normal, ret}`. Note s' IS the body's final

  bridge: if the machine first reaches `halt` at exactly step K (the `hne`
```

### How E7 went (the last `sorry`)
`entry_run_sim` assembles the top-level machine run by mirroring the `lower_sim_cf` `call` case, minus marshalling/ret-stores, with `holes = []` and the return address = the halt pad `codeBase + 4`:

- IL-side inversion (`run_inv`) + layout/`hfn` harvest (`fn_hfn`, `layoutOf_decomp`, `stub_emitted`, and the Phase-2 discharge layer)

- memory conclusion via `StInv` c4; `hne` via `NoHalt` chained over stub·prologue·body·epilogue, feeding `runFuel_eq_stepN`

The debugging was all namespace/lemma-name plumbing (`set` is Mathlib-only; `RA`/`totalFrame` needed `open`; `run_inv` lives in `LowIR.Prog`; `getD → getElem` via `List.getD_eq_getElem?_getD`) plus one `rw` direction on the epilogue memory-preservation — no structural surprises; the ledger in RESUME-ENTRY §4 held.

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:38:47; model 37.7s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Here's what `prog_sim` actually says, in plain terms. It's a **compiler-correctness / simulation theorem** relating two worlds:

- **IL world** — `LowIR.Prog.run P … entry args … sp0 … = some s'`: the abstract D7/D8 interpreter calls function `entry` with arguments `args` and returns a final IL state `s'`.
- **Machine world** — `runFuel L.haltPc k m0`: the *actual* RV64I `step` function iterated on a real machine state `m0` that has the compiled blob loaded, until the pc reaches the halt pad.

## The claim

> **If** the machine is set up to run the compiled image of `P` (blob installed, pc at `codeBase`, sp = `sp0`, arguments in `a0, a1, …`), **and** the IL interpreter says `entry(args)` terminates with result `s'`, **then** the real RV64I machine, run forward, **halts** and agrees with the IL on the observable result.

Concretely the conclusion (`Main.lean:357–361`) gives a step count `k` with three properties:

1. **Halts** — `(runFuel L.haltPc k m0).pc = L.haltPc`: the machine reaches the halt pad.
2. **Right return values** — for each `j < fd.rvc`, machine register `a_j` (reg `10+j`) `= s'.rget rets[j]`: every return register holds exactly the IL's return value.
3. **Right memory** — for every address `a` *outside the blob and outside the machine stack*, `s'.mem a = (runFuel … k m0).mem a`: the machine's final memory matches the IL's on all user-visible bytes.

So the concrete compiled RV64I program computes, register-for-register and byte-for-byte, what the D7/D8 IL semantics says `entry(args)` computes.

## The preconditions it needs (the honest scope)

The hypotheses (`Main.lean:336–356`) are the "fair setup" conditions: `hinst`/`hmem` — the blob really is loaded and initial memory matches the data image off the private region; `hpc`/`hsp`/`hargc`/`hargs` — pc/sp/arg registers in the calling convention; `hpre : SimPre` — stack/blob geometry sane (alignment, disjointness, blob fits); `hbr` — the compiler's branch-span well-formedness; `haccess` — the entry body only touches memory outside the blob (the footprint/borrow side condition); `hrun` — the IL run *terminates within `fuel`*.

That last one makes it **partial correctness relative to IL termination**: whenever the IL says the program produces a result, the machine reproduces it. It's per-entry-point, over the frozen D7/D8 `-O0` compiler.

## Why the axiom line matters

`#print axioms prog_sim = [propext, Quot.sound]` — only Lean's two standard foundational axioms. No `sorry`, no `Classical.choice`, and crucially no `native_decide`/`Lean.ofReduceBool` (which would mean "trust Lean's compiled evaluator on this program"). The correspondence is proved by structural reasoning about `decode`/`step`, not by running anything.

(`entry_run_sim` at `Main.lean:64` is the same statement phrased with raw `stepN` iteration plus the "no early halt" clause; `prog_sim` composes it with `runFuel_eq_stepN` to phrase the result in terms of the real fuel-bounded machine loop.)

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:41:36; model 42.9s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Good question — they're the **same genre** but not the same theorem. Same conclusion skeleton (`∃ k`, machine `runFuel` reaches a terminal pc, code still installed, IL final state agrees with the machine), but they live at very different altitudes, and one is proved while the other never was.

| | `Core.compile_sim` (T1) | `ProgSim.prog_sim` |
|---|---|---|
| **Subject** | `p : Stmt` — one flat statement block in the *old* Core IL | `P : Program` — a whole D7/D8 program |
| **`compile`** | `compile p` = a flat instruction list | `layoutOf P entry …` — fnTab, data segment, frames, stub, halt pad |
| **Functions / calls** | none | function env `P.env`, real `call`, prologue/epilogue, frames |
| **Data segment** | none | `P.data`, installed data image, `dbaseOf`/`dposOf` |
| **Entry convention** | none — "just run this block" | named `entry`, args in `a0…`, returns in `rvc` return regs |
| **Agreement** | `Agree` = **all** registers equal + mem off code window | only **return** regs match; mem off blob **and** off stack; `StInv`/`MachPriv`/`OffPriv` private-region discipline |
| **Footprint** | `NoSelfModify` — admitted end-to-end **approximation** (docstring flags it as v1) | `MemAccOff` — the real per-body borrow/footprint condition |
| **Status** | `sorry` (Core.lean:383) | **proved, `[propext, Quot.sound]`** |

So the differences aren't cosmetic:

- **Different language.** T1 is about a flat `Stmt` with no notion of functions, calls, data, or a stack frame. `prog_sim` is about the whole-program D7/D8 IR that has all of those — that's the "compiler cut" the project did after T1 was written.
- **Different (and correctly weaker) agreement.** T1's `Agree` demands *every* register match — which is actually too strong for a real calling convention (a prologue/epilogue legitimately clobbers scratch and callee-saved regs). `prog_sim` only pins the return registers, and its memory separation is the refined private-region story (`StInv`, `MachStack`, `OffPriv`) rather than "off the code window."
- **T1's separation hypothesis is admitted.** `NoSelfModify` is explicitly a v1 approximation; `prog_sim` replaces it with the real footprint hypothesis `haccess : MemAccOff …` that the borrow checker will eventually discharge.

The honest framing: T1 was the **original aspiration** — a well-formed statement written to show "the design *admits* a forward-simulation theorem," with the proof deliberately deferred. The IL and compiler were then redesigned into Prog, and `prog_sim` is the **realized successor** at a strictly richer altitude, actually proved axiom-clean. It is *not* a proof of `compile_sim`; `compile_sim` remains an open `sorry` about the older, simpler IL that the project has moved past (the campaign memory explicitly marks it "do NOT prove — superseded").

So: essentially the same *idea* (compiler correctness as forward simulation), but `prog_sim` is the stronger, whole-program, actually-proved version — not the same statement.

Yes — the D7/D8 **Prog** dialect of LowIR has real function calls. The `PStmt` inductive (`Prog.lean:57`) has:

```lean
| call (argc rvc : Nat) (f : Name)
       (args : Vector Reg argc) (rets : Vector Reg rvc)
```

That's a genuine ABI call: call function named `f`, passing `argc` argument registers, receiving `rvc` return registers. It's backed by:

- a **function environment** `P.env : List (Name × FunDef)` — a whole program is a table of named functions, not one flat block;
- **frames** — `frameEnter` sets up the callee's stack frame (`frameReg`, `frameSize`, the frame-padding oracle `pad`), and the compiler emits real **prologue/epilogue** (`prologueI`/`epilogueI`: `addi sp, sp, -frame`; save `ra`; park params into slots; …; restore; `jalr`);
- the lowering primitive **`callf f`** (`Compile.lean:61`) = `jal ra, <fnPos f>`, with argument marshalling (`marshalI`) before and return-store (`retStoresI`) after.

Proving that `call` case correct (the prologue/epilogue/marshalling simulation, the dead-hole `StInv` repair for memory after return) was Phase 5 of the campaign — the largest single piece, and `entry_run_sim` that I just closed is essentially the top-level call (`main`'s invocation) assembled from those same atoms.

One distinction worth flagging, since it connects to your last question: there are **two IL layers** here.

- The old flat **Core `Stmt`** (what `compile_sim`/T1 is about) has **no calls** — just straight-line ops + `ife`/`while`/`block`. That's why T1 needs no frames, no calling convention, no data segment.
- The **Prog** dialect (`prog_sim`) is the one with calls, functions, and data.

(There's also an intermediate **Ctrl** dialect at `Ctrl.lean:44` with a simpler `call (g : Stmt)` — "run the callee's body, catch its `ret`" — used in the hex0 proofs; it models the call/return control flow without the register-level ABI that Prog's `call` commits to.)

```
75:def decode (w : BitVec 32) : Instr :=

=== compileProgT signature ===
def compileProgT (P : Program) (entry : Name) :
    Option (List Instr × List (Name × Nat) × List (Name × Nat)) :=
  if !(wfProgram P && P.env.all (fun nf => fnOk nf.2)
       && (List.lookup entry P.env).isSome
       && P.data.all (fun d => d.2.length < 2 ^ 22))    -- cref/clen synth range
       -- NOTE: bound tightened 2^23 → 2^22 (2026-07-05). `clen` synthesizes the
       -- data length via `synthConst` (no range check), whose hi immediate is
       -- `synthHi len = ⌊(len+2048)/4096⌋`; that hits 2048 for `len ∈ [2^23−2048,
       -- 2^23)`, overflowing `BitVec.ofInt 12` (wraps to −2048 ⇒ wrong length).
       -- 2^22 sits well inside the synthesizable band; discharges ProgSim `hdat`.
  then none
  else
    let segs : List (Name × List SymInstr) :=
      (P.env.mapM (fun nf => do pure (nf.1, ← compileFun P.data nf.2)) : M _).run' 0
    let (flat, lbls, fns, codeEnd) :=
      layout (("", [.callf entry, .ins (jal0 0)]) :: segs) 0
    -- SAME layout function as the IL harness (`Prog.dbaseOf`/`installData`
    -- use `dataOffsetsFrom 0`); correspondence proved in `Prog.dataSegment_at`.
    let dats := dataOffsetsFrom (pad8 codeEnd) P.data
    ((flat.mapM (resolveOne lbls fns dats)).map List.flatten).map
      (fun is => (is, fns.filter (fun f => f.1 != ""), dats))

def compileProg (P : Program) (entry : Name) : Option (List Instr) :=
  (compileProgT P entry).map (·.1)
```

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:48:37; model 37.1s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Here's the actual stack `prog_sim` sits in — two endpoints with the compiler pipeline between them, all inside the repo's four-layer tower (`Spec / RawAsm / LowIR / LowSSA`).

## The proved link: Prog IR ⟹ RV64I machine

```
  LowIR.Prog.run  (source: D7/D8 Prog IR big-step semantics)   Prog.lean:438
        │
        │   compiled by  LowIR.Compile.compileProgT            Compile.lean:316
        │     compileFun (per function)
        │       → lower    : PStmt → [SymInstr]   (symbolic labels/fn/data refs)
        │       → layout   : assign byte positions, build label/fn/data tables
        │       → resolveOne: SymInstr → Rv64i.Instr (labels/fns/data → PC-rel offsets)
        │     layoutOf wraps this into a Layout (codeBase/stackLo/segStart/blobLen + data image)
        ▼
  RawAsm.Rv64i    (target: the trusted RV64I machine)          RawAsm/Rv64i.lean
     State · Instr (16 encodings) · decode/encode/fetch32 · step · runFuel
```

`prog_sim` is the **forward simulation** between these two: `Prog.run P … entry args … = some s'` (the IL evaluates the program) implies `Rv64i.runFuel L.haltPc k m0` (the real machine, stepping decoded bytes) halts with the IL's return values in `a0…` and its memory off the blob/stack.

## Where each piece lives

- **Source — `LowIR.Prog`** is the top of the *LowIR family*, which is a stack of dialects of increasing structure:
  - `LowIR.Core` (`Core.lean`) — flat `Stmt`, no calls (this is what the *unproved* `compile_sim` targets);
  - `LowIR.Ctrl` (`Ctrl.lean`) — structured control: `break`/`continue`, `call (g : Stmt)` (call-a-body);
  - `LowIR.Prog` (`Prog.lean`, imports Ctrl) — **functions, frames, data segment, the ABI `call argc rvc f args rets`**. This is the one `prog_sim` starts from.
- **Compiler — `LowIR.Compile`** (imports Prog): the frozen `-O0` `compileProgT`. The `decode (encode i) = i` round-trip (EncodeFacts, Phase 1) is the glue that lets the proof cross from resolved `Rv64i.Instr` through bytes and back to what `fetch32`/`decode` produces.
- **Target — `RawAsm.Rv64i`**: the **trusted computing base**. `step`/`decode` are the semantics everything else is proved *relative to*; nothing below it is verified — it's the spec of the hardware.

## The surrounding tower (not in `prog_sim`'s proved stack)

- **`LowSSA`** (`LowSSA.lean`, `SSAProof/`) — the SSA IR, conceptually *above* Prog. The ladder's planned **SSA→Prog lowering simulation** is a **future** link — not yet proved; today SSA is used independently for the hex0 proofs.
- **`Spec.lean`** — the top specification layer.
- Note `hex0`'s decoder argument is proved three times over — at the RawAsm, Ctrl, and SSA altitudes — as siblings, not yet composed through Prog.

So today `prog_sim` proves exactly **one rung**: the D7/D8 Prog IR compiles correctly to trusted RV64I. The rungs above it (SSA → Prog, and a Spec → SSA story) are the post-campaign ladder in `docs/PROOF-COMPLEXITY.md`.

### 👤 **User**  ·  20:51:03; waited 2m10s

does prog_sim bridge the complexity to riscv asm?

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:51:24; model 38.4s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Yes — that's precisely its job, and it's worth being exact about *which* complexity it carries and where the bridge is anchored at each end.

## What it bridges

`prog_sim` moves the hard, low-level reasoning **out of per-program proofs and into one once-proved theorem**. All of the following live *inside* `prog_sim`'s proof and are discharged a single time:

- **instruction decode / fetch** — `Installed` + `decode_at`: the 32-bit word at each address recombines (`fetch32`) and `decode`s to the intended instruction, via the `decode (encode i) = i` round-trip (EncodeFacts);
- **PC-relative offset arithmetic** — `jump_lands`, `signExtend_ofInt_13/21`, `cond_taken`: every branch/jal lands where the structured control flow says it should;
- **the calling convention & frames** — prologue/epilogue, `frameEnter`, argument marshalling, return-store, the stack-slot memory model (`StInv`);
- **stack/blob separation** — `MachPriv`/`MachStack`/`OffPriv`: what memory the machine may touch vs. what stays stable.

That's the "flat-altitude" cost that `Hex0/Refine.lean` pays *per program*. `prog_sim` amortizes it into the compiler, so a functional-correctness proof done at the **structured Prog IR** altitude (register/memory transformers, `while`-invariants — no PCs, no offsets, no decode) **transports to the concrete RV64I bytes** by composition. Prove `strlen` correct once over `Prog.exec`, compose with `prog_sim`, and you get a statement about the real machine registers after real `step`s.

## Where the bridge is anchored

**Bottom end — yes, it reaches RISC-V asm, but to the *modelled* ISA.** It bottoms out at `RawAsm.Rv64i.step`/`decode`/`runFuel` — the actual small-step machine over decoded bytes (16 RV64I encodings). So it genuinely lands on executing the concrete encoded instructions, not a hand-wave. But `Rv64i.step` is the **trusted computing base**: `prog_sim` does *not* validate that model against silicon or an external golden spec (sail / riscv-coq). That cross-check is separate project work, outside this theorem.

**Top end — it starts at Prog, not at a high-level Spec.** The bridge begins at the D7/D8 Prog IR, not at SSA or a source-level specification. So `prog_sim` is **one rung**: Prog IR ⟹ trusted RV64I. The rungs that would extend the bridge *upward* — SSA → Prog lowering, and Spec → SSA — are the future ladder in `docs/PROOF-COMPLEXITY.md`, not yet proved.

## The honest caveats on "bridge"

- **Partial correctness relative to IL termination** — the guarantee is conditional on `Prog.run` returning a result within fuel (`hrun`).
- **Side conditions must hold** — correct loading (`Installed`/`hmem`), the footprint/borrow condition (`haccess : MemAccOff …`, which the planned borrow checker will discharge), and stack/blob geometry (`SimPre`).
- **Frozen `-O0` compiler** — it's the specific `compileProgT`, per entry point.

So: yes, `prog_sim` is the load-bearing bridge that carries decode/offset/frame complexity down to executing RV64I instructions — a complete rung from the structured Prog IR to the trusted machine. It just isn't the *whole* span yet: the trusted-model gap sits below it, and the SSA/Spec rungs sit above it.

### 👤 **User**  ·  20:53:05; waited 1m24s

how hard would it be to add optimizations? currently the registers run on local stack always

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:53:47; model 1m04s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Good architectural question. The short version: **optimizations that live *above* the simulation are cheap; optimizations that change the *lowering* range from moderate to a near-rewrite — and register allocation specifically is the expensive end, precisely because "always spill to a slot" is what makes the current proof tractable.**

## Why "registers always on the stack" is load-bearing for the proof

The `-O0` lowering spills every IL register around every op: `loadSlot rs T0` → compute in `T0`/`T1` → `storeSlot rd T0`. Each IL register `r` is really the memory slot at `slotOff r = 8*(1+r)` in the frame; only `T0`/`T1` (and sp/ra/a0…) are ever physical.

That uniformity is what makes the simulation relation simple. `StInv`'s slot-agreement conjunct is a **fixed, program-point-independent abstraction map**:

> for every IL register `r ≤ maxRegF`, the machine slot at `slotAddr r` holds `s.rget r`.

The machine register file (outside scratch/convention) is *irrelevant* to the relation. Every IL `rget`/`rset` corresponds to exactly one machine slot load/store, and `StInv_store_slot` re-establishes all conjuncts after each one. There's no "which value is in which register right now" to track. That's the entire reason `lower_sim_cf` was provable at human scale.

## The cost tiers

**Tier 1 — optimize *above* `prog_sim` (cheap, and the whole point of having it).**
Prog→Prog (or SSA→Prog, once that rung lands) transforms proved as `exec`-preserving IL rewrites: constant folding, dead-code elimination, algebraic simplification, strength reduction, inlining. These need **zero** machine/offset/decode reasoning — you prove semantic preservation at the structured altitude, then compose with `prog_sim` unchanged. This is exactly the leverage the bridge buys, and it's where I'd put most optimization effort. Difficulty: a normal verified-rewrite proof per pass, no proof-infrastructure change.

**Tier 2 — reduce spills but keep the slot model (moderate).**
E.g. skip the `store`-then-reload when a freshly produced value is consumed immediately (value already in `T0`). This perturbs the emitted instruction sequence, so you re-prove the affected `lower_sim_cf` cases and their offset arithmetic — but the *global* slot invariant survives (every register still has a canonical slot; you've just elided redundant traffic at specific points). Difficulty: real work per optimization, but the simulation relation doesn't change shape.

**Tier 3 — real register allocation (expensive).**
Keeping IL values in physical (callee-/caller-saved) registers across statements breaks the fixed abstraction map. `StInv` would have to become a **program-point-dependent location map** `Reg → (PhysReg | Slot)` that changes at every PC, with spill/reload insertion proved correct at the boundaries. That's the classic hardest pass in verified compilers — CompCert doesn't even prove its allocator directly; it uses **translation validation** (a checker) for exactly this reason. Difficulty: effectively a rewrite of the simulation relation and most leaf lemmas.

## The escape hatch for Tier 3

If you want aggressive codegen without paying Tier-3 proof cost: **translation validation.** Don't prove the optimizer; prove a *checker* that validates each compiled output against its input (à la CompCert's `Allocation`). You prove the checker sound once, and the optimizer itself can be arbitrarily clever and unverified. For register allocation this is usually the sane path, and it fits cleanly here — the checker would re-derive a per-point location map and confirm slot/reg agreement, then feed the same `prog_sim`-style conclusion.

## Bottom line

The architecture already steers you toward the cheap direction: `prog_sim` carries decode/offset/frame complexity down once, so **push optimizations up to the IL** and compose. The "always spill" decision was the right call for getting to a proved compiler — it deliberately trades code quality for a trivial abstraction map. Undoing it in the codegen (real reg-alloc) is the one genuinely hard change, and even then translation validation lets you sidestep proving the allocator directly.

One concrete near-term note: the slot model also caps IL registers at `maxRegF` distinct frame slots, and the prologue zeroes them — so any Tier-3 work interacts with the frame-layout and `SimPre` geometry too, not just `StInv`. That coupling is another reason to keep the first wave of optimizations at the IL altitude where none of it is in scope.

```
## What the battery validates (all `#guard`, executable)

The user's exact `if` example (a `.never` arm returning directly, out register
uninitialized on that path); value-producing blocks; `sumTo` with a
guard-exit that *carries* the loop-carried accumulator + the zero-trip case;
`brk 1` escaping an `ife` into the enclosing `while` while `cont 0` passes
through the `ife` unshifted; multi-value returns with no return registers;
D8 frames + recursion + stack-overflow trip under SSA names; and 7 checker
negatives (double def, use-before-def, arm-local escaping its join, brk arity
mismatch, fall-through loop body, missing return, dead code after `.never`).
```

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:57:00; model 46.3s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Short answer: **there's no theorem named "no-read-undef," and at the level `prog_sim` operates it isn't a property that even arises — the model has no undefined values to read.** The nearest real guarantees are *static* and live one layer up (SSA), with the borrow checker as the planned piece. Let me be precise about why.

## Why it doesn't exist as a runtime theorem here

The Prog IL state (and the RV64I machine state) are **total functions, not partial**:

- `St.regs : Reg → Word` and `St.mem : Word → Byte` — total. `rget` returns `0` for reg 0 and `regs i` otherwise; there is no `Option`, no poison, no undef (`Prog.lean:92–105`).
- The prologue additionally **zero-initializes** the frame: `frameEnter` builds `st0.mem = zeroRange (installData …) …` and non-param registers default via `parkFold (fun _ => 0)`.

So reading an unwritten slot or register is deterministically well-defined (concretely `0` in a fresh frame), not garbage. There is nothing to "trip on." This is the same posture as the CakeML note in the third-party docs (`CAKEML-DESIGN.md:86`): *"Fail (undefined behavior) never needs to be improved"* — the low semantics is total, so a UB-avoidance / no-read-of-undef obligation is vacuous at that altitude. `prog_sim` therefore neither states nor needs it.

## Where definedness actually lives — and it *is* partly here

The concept becomes real one rung up, at the **SSA layer** (`LowSSA`), where a value genuinely can be "unset on a path." And that's handled by a **static checker**, not a runtime proof:

- The SSA well-formedness checker already rejects **use-before-def** — it's one of the 7 checker negatives the SSA battery validates (`LOWIR-SSA-EXPERIMENT.md`: *"double def, use-before-def, arm-local escaping its join, …"*). The doc's "out register uninitialized on that path" example is exactly the case it's built to catch.

So the "don't read something never written" guarantee exists as a **static SSA invariant**, present today in the experiment, not as a semantic theorem over `prog_sim`.

## The planned piece: the borrow checker (Ext. 5)

The roadmap item closest to what you're asking is the **borrow checker over the SSA IR** (`PROOF-COMPLEXITY.md §4`, `LOWIR-SSA-EXPERIMENT.md:166`). Its job is to *discharge* the `MemAccOff` / `execT` / `Wf` hypotheses that `prog_sim` currently takes as assumptions — i.e. prove statically that a program's memory accesses stay within its live borrows (disjointness, off the private region). That's **spatial** memory safety (the Tree-Borrows-style discipline in `MEMORY-BORROWS.md`), and combined with SSA's use-before-def check it's the definedness-and-safety story. But it's phrased as *borrow well-formedness*, not as a standalone "no-read-undef" deliverable — and it's **planned, not yet built** (it's a post-`prog_sim` ladder rung).

## If you specifically wanted a semantic "reads only touch initialized memory"

That would map onto strengthening `MemAccOff` (the footprint condition `haccess` in `prog_sim`) from "accesses stay off-private" to "reads only hit previously-written or borrowed bytes." It's expressible in the existing borrow vocabulary and would be the borrow checker's natural output — but it is not a separate named goal in the current plan. So: **not here, and only implicitly planned** — folded into the borrow-checker rung rather than tracked as its own proof.
